========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, I mean… I’m there, I think. I can hear them yelling sometimes, like “move” or “get back,” but it’s like the forklift beeping gets lined up with my arms, if that makes sense. I’ll be standing there with my arms up, and I know it looks stupid or dangerous, but it’s hard to just stop.

Sometimes if someone grabs my shoulder or waves me over, I can move, but other times I kind of keep doing it—like the beeping sound, or the hand signal the guy made. Then I’ll go stack boxes in those little patterns, and they’re all yelling, and I know they don’t want it that way, but I’m already in it. It’s like when you start sorting coins by the year and then you remember the state quarters—sorry, I know that’s not what you asked. But yeah, I freeze and copy stuff, and it’s hard to switch back right away.

It’s not really clear to me why.\n\n[Last Question]\nBefore you make the beeping sounds or move your arms like that, do you ever feel a warning in your body—like a need to do it, an itch, or something building up that goes away once you do the movement or sound?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 20 / laborer_or_freight_stock_or_material_mover / some_college\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior\nmanifestation: \"I guess they brought me in because at the warehouse I keep doing things like freezing with my arms up by the forklift, copying the beeping sounds and hand signals, or moving boxes in strange little patterns even when everyone is yelling for me to stop.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. I don’t really notice an itch or anything like that first. It’s more like… I’m just there, and then I’m doing it. Like by the forklift, I’ll freeze with my arms up, or I’m copying the beeping, or doing the hand signals, and people are yelling at me to stop but I’m already sort of caught in it.

And then the boxes—sometimes I start putting them in these weird little patterns, not how they’re supposed to go. It’s not like I feel better after, exactly. Like by the forklift, I’ll freeze with my arms up, or I’m copying the beeping, or doing the hand signals, and people are yelling at me to stop but I’m already sort of caught in it.\n\nAnd then the boxes—sometimes I start putting them in these weird little patterns, not how they’re supposed to go. It’s not really clear to me why.\n\nDoctor: Before you make the beeping sounds or move your arms like that, do you ever feel a warning in your body—like a need to do it, an itch, or something building up that goes away once you do the movement or sound?\n\nPatient: I don’t really notice an itch or anything like that first. It’s more like… I’m just there, and then I’m doing it. Like by the forklift, I’ll freeze with my arms up, or I’m copying the beeping, or doing the hand signals, and people are yelling at me to stop but I’m already sort of caught in it.\n\nAnd then the boxes—sometimes I start putting them in these weird little patterns, not how they’re supposed to go. It’s not like I feel better after, exactly. It just happens, and then it’s hard to stop. The warehouse lights are really bright too, they buzz sometimes like those old school lights, the kind in classrooms—sorry. But yeah, no warning I can point to.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work at the warehouse, do you ever find yourself copying sounds you hear or movements you see other people make, even if you don’t mean to?\n\nPatient: I mean, mostly they brought me in because of the warehouse stuff, I think. Like freezing with my arms up by the forklift, or copying the beeping sounds and the hand signals, and then I’m moving boxes into these little patterns even while people are yelling at me to stop.\n\nOutside of work… I don’t know, I’m trying to think. The forklift beeps are kind of like when a microwave keeps going and nobody opens it, which is annoying—sorry. But the big thing people keep pointing at is what happens at work. That’s where it gets bad and everybody notices.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself making sudden sounds or movements, like blinking, clearing your throat, or shrugging your shoulders, when you're not at work—like when you're at home watching TV or talking with someone?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know about the TV thing… like blinking or throat clearing, I don’t think that’s what people complain about. Mostly they brought me in because of the warehouse.\n\nI keep doing these things there, like I freeze with my arms up by the forklift, or I start copying the beeping sounds and the hand signals. And then I move the boxes into these strange little patterns, even when everyone’s yelling at me to stop. It’s like that’s the main thing everybody noticed. The beeping kind of gets stuck in the air there—sorry, I know you asked about home. I just don’t really notice it the same way at home.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don’t know if it’s like an urge in my body. It’s more like… at the warehouse it just starts happening. twitches, or sudden sounds like grunting or throat clearing in other places outside of work—like in a store, on the street, or somewhere else?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Like, in a store I’m usually just trying to get in and out, and the lights are too much sometimes—well, not the lights, I mean, I just don’t pay attention to whether I’m blinking or shrugging.\n\nNobody’s told me I’m doing that kind of thing, like grunting or clearing my throat. I don’t think so. I mostly only hear about the stuff at the warehouse, so outside of that I don’t really know.\n\nDoctor: When you're about to make those beeping sounds or move your arms like you described, do you ever feel a strong urge or a strange sensation in your body that makes you feel like you have to do it?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s like an urge in my body. It’s more like… at the warehouse it just starts happening. I’ll be by the forklift and my arms sort of go up and I freeze there, or I start copying the beeping sounds and the hand signals, and then the boxes—sometimes I start putting them in these little patterns.\n\nPeople yell at me to stop, but it’s hard to break out of it right then. I’m not thinking, “I need to do this because of a feeling,” exactly. It’s just like the beeping and the movements get caught up in me.\n\nDoctor: When you're not at the warehouse—like when you're at home or somewhere else—do you ever feel a strange feeling in your body before you make a sound or move in a way that you can't control?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I don’t notice that kind of thing when I’m home. If I’m on the couch or making food or whatever, I’m not thinking, “oh, there’s that feeling before I move.” It’s more like I’m just doing normal stuff, and then I get distracted by—like my neighbor’s dog barks at the same time every night, which is annoying, but that’s not the same thing.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I get a strange body feeling before making sounds or moving outside the warehouse. Or if I do, I haven’t noticed it.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever found yourself repeating words, sounds, or movements that you hear or see other people do in places outside of the warehouse—like at a store, on the street, or when you're at home?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Outside? I don’t know… I keep thinking about the warehouse because that’s what everyone keeps saying. Like by the forklift I’ll just freeze with my arms up, or I start making the beeping sounds back, doing the hand signals, and then the boxes end up in these little patterns.

It’s not like I’m trying to make a joke or anything. People yell at me to stop, and I hear them, but I’m kind of stuck on it. At a store or home… I can’t really think of a time like that. It’s mostly the warehouse stuff they brought me in for.

  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF84.3, F98.4, F98.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, thank you for coming in. What brings you here today?\n\nPatient: Uh… I guess they brought me in because of stuff at the warehouse. Like, I’ll be by the forklift and just kind of freeze there with my arms up, and people get mad because it’s not safe, but the beeping gets stuck in my head, you know? Those forklifts beep like the microwave my aunt used to have, except hers had this cracked button and—\n\nAnyway, I guess I also copy the beeping sounds and the hand signals the guys do. And sometimes I move boxes into these little patterns, not how they want them stacked. They yell at me to stop, but I’m already doing it, and then it’s hard to… switch back, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about those times when you freeze by the forklift? Are you aware of what’s happening when it happens, and can you move if someone tries to redirect you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… I’m there, I think. I can hear them yelling sometimes, like “move” or “get back,” but it’s like the forklift beeping gets lined up with my arms, if that makes sense. I’ll be standing there with my arms up, and I know it looks stupid or dangerous, but it’s hard to just stop.\n\nSometimes if someone grabs my shoulder or waves me over, I can move, but other times I kind of keep doing it—like the beeping sound, or the hand signal the guy made. Then I’ll go stack boxes in those little patterns, and they’re all yelling, and I know they don’t want it that way, but I’m already in it. It’s like when you start sorting coins by the year and then you remember the state quarters—sorry, I know that’s not what you asked. But yeah, I freeze and copy stuff, and it’s hard to switch back right away.\n\nDoctor: When you find yourself making the beeping sounds or copying the hand signals, does it feel like something you have to do to relieve a thought or feeling, or does it just happen without a clear reason?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s like… to make a thought go away. It’s not like I’m thinking, “I have to beep or something bad happens.” It more just happens, I guess.\n\nLike at the warehouse, I’ll be by the forklift and I freeze with my arms up, or I start copying the beeping and the hand signals, and then the boxes end up in those little patterns even though everyone’s yelling at me to stop. It’s embarrassing because I can hear them, but I’m already doing it. It’s not really clear to me why.\n\nDoctor: Before you make the beeping sounds or move your arms like that, do you ever feel a warning in your body—like a need to do it, an itch, or something building up that goes away once you do the movement or sound?\n\nPatient: I don’t really notice an itch or anything like that first. It’s more like… I’m just there, and then I’m doing it. Like by the forklift, I’ll freeze with my arms up, or I’m copying the beeping, or doing the hand signals, and people are yelling at me to stop but I’m already sort of caught in it.\n\nAnd then the boxes—sometimes I start putting them in these weird little patterns, not how they’re supposed to go. It’s not like I feel better after, exactly. It just happens, and then it’s hard to stop. The warehouse lights are really bright too, they buzz sometimes like those old school lights, the kind in classrooms—sorry. But yeah, no warning I can point to.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work at the warehouse, do you ever find yourself copying sounds you hear or movements you see other people make, even if you don’t mean to?\n\nPatient: I mean, mostly they brought me in because of the warehouse stuff, I think. Like freezing with my arms up by the forklift, or copying the beeping sounds and the hand signals, and then I’m moving boxes into these little patterns even while people are yelling at me to stop.\n\nOutside of work… I don’t know, I’m trying to think. The forklift beeps are kind of like when a microwave keeps going and nobody opens it, which is annoying—sorry. But the big thing people keep pointing at is what happens at work. That’s where it gets bad and everybody notices.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself making sudden sounds or movements, like blinking, clearing your throat, or shrugging your shoulders, when you're not at work—like when you're at home watching TV or talking with someone?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know about the TV thing… like blinking or throat clearing, I don’t think that’s what people complain about. Mostly they brought me in because of the warehouse.\n\nI keep doing these things there, like I freeze with my arms up by the forklift, or I start copying the beeping sounds and the hand signals. And then I move the boxes into these strange little patterns, even when everyone’s yelling at me to stop. It’s like that’s the main thing everybody noticed. The beeping kind of gets stuck in the air there—sorry, I know you asked about home. I just don’t really notice it the same way at home.\n\nDoctor: When you're at home or in other places outside the warehouse, do you ever notice yourself making sudden movements—like blinking, shrugging, or facial twitches—or sudden sounds like grunting or clearing your throat without meaning to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about blinking or grunting, not really… I mean, nobody at home says that’s the problem.\n\nThey brought me in more because at the warehouse I keep doing those things, like I’ll freeze with my arms up near the forklift, or I start copying the beeping and the hand signals. And then with the boxes, I move them into these weird little patterns even when people are yelling at me to stop. It’s mostly that stuff. The warehouse just kind of gets… loud and all lined up wrong, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Even if it doesn’t happen at home, have you ever noticed yourself making sudden movements like blinking, shrugging, or facial twitches, or sudden sounds like grunting or throat clearing in other places outside of work—like in a store, on the street, or somewhere else?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Like, in a store I’m usually just trying to get in and out, and the lights are too much sometimes—well, not the lights, I mean, I just don’t pay attention to whether I’m blinking or shrugging.\n\nNobody’s told me I’m doing that kind of thing, like grunting or clearing my throat. I don’t think so. I mostly only hear about the stuff at the warehouse, so outside of that I don’t really know.\n\nDoctor: When you're about to make those beeping sounds or move your arms like you described, do you ever feel a strong urge or a strange sensation in your body that makes you feel like you have to do it?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s like an urge in my body. It’s more like… at the warehouse it just starts happening. I’ll be by the forklift and my arms sort of go up and I freeze there, or I start copying the beeping sounds and the hand signals, and then the boxes—sometimes I start putting them in these little patterns.\n\nPeople yell at me to stop, but it’s hard to break out of it right then. I’m not thinking, “I need to do this because of a feeling,” exactly. It’s just like the beeping and the movements get caught up in me.\n\nDoctor: When you're not at the warehouse—like when you're at home or somewhere else—do you ever feel a strange feeling in your body before you make a sound or move in a way that you can't control?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I don’t notice that kind of thing when I’m home. If I’m on the couch or making food or whatever, I’m not thinking, “oh, there’s that feeling before I move.” It’s more like I’m just doing normal stuff, and then I get distracted by—like my neighbor’s dog barks at the same time every night, which is annoying, but that’s not the same thing.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I get a strange body feeling before making sounds or moving outside the warehouse. Or if I do, I haven’t noticed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeating words, sounds, or movements that you hear or see other people do in places outside of the warehouse—like at a store, on the street, or when you're at home?\n\nPatient: I mean… outside the warehouse, I don’t really know. The thing people keep bringing up is at work, like with the forklift. I’ll freeze with my arms up, or I end up copying the beeping and the hand signals, and then I start putting boxes in these little patterns.\n\nIt’s weird because everybody’s yelling and it’s like I can hear them, but I’m also stuck on the beeping or the way the boxes line up. At home I’m mostly just… I don’t know, trying to sit still or eat something. Nobody’s said I’m copying them there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeating words, sounds, or movements that you hear or see other people do in places outside of the warehouse—like at a store, on the street, or when you're at home?\n\nPatient: Outside? I don’t know… I keep thinking about the warehouse because that’s what everyone keeps saying. Like by the forklift I’ll just freeze with my arms up, or I start making the beeping sounds back, doing the hand signals, and then the boxes end up in these little patterns.\n\nIt’s not like I’m trying to make a joke or anything. People yell at me to stop, and I hear them, but I’m kind of stuck on it. At a store or home… I can’t really think of a time like that. Or if I do, I haven’t noticed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeating words, sounds, or movements that you hear or see other people do in places outside of the warehouse—like at a store, on the street, or when you're at home?\n\nPatient: I mean… outside the warehouse, I don’t really know. The thing people keep bringing up is at work, like with the forklift. I’ll freeze with my arms up, or I end up copying the beeping and the hand signals, and then I start putting boxes in these little patterns.\n\nIt’s weird because everybody’s yelling and it’s like I can hear them, but I’m also stuck on the beeping or the way the boxes line up. I don’t know… I keep thinking about the warehouse because that’s what everyone keeps saying. It’s embarrassing because I can hear them, but I’m already doing it. It’s not really clear to me why.\n\nDoctor: Before you make the beeping sounds or move your arms like that, do you ever feel a warning in your body—like a need to do it, an itch, or something building up that goes away once you do the movement or sound?\n\nPatient: I don’t really notice an itch or anything like that first. It’s more like… I’m just there, and then I’m doing it. Like by the forklift, I’ll freeze with my arms up, or I’m copying the beeping, or doing the hand signals, and people are yelling at me to stop but I’m already sort of caught in it.\n\nAnd then the boxes—sometimes I start putting them in these weird little patterns, not how they’re supposed to go. It’s not like I feel better after, exactly. It just happens, and then it’s hard to stop. The warehouse lights are really bright too, they buzz sometimes like those old school lights, the kind in classrooms—sorry. But yeah, no warning I can point to.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work at the warehouse, do you ever find yourself copying sounds you hear or movements you see other people make, even if you don’t mean to?\n\nPatient: I mean, mostly they brought me in because of the warehouse stuff, I think. Like freezing with my arms up by the forklift, or copying the beeping sounds and the hand signals, and then I’m moving boxes into these little patterns even while people are yelling at me to stop.\n\nOutside of work… I don’t know, I’m trying to think. The forklift beeps are kind of like when a microwave keeps going and nobody opens it, which is annoying—sorry. But the big thing people keep pointing at is what happens at work. That’s where it gets bad and everybody notices.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself making sudden sounds or movements, like blinking, clearing your throat, or shrugging your shoulders, when you're not at work—like when you're at home watching TV or talking with someone?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know about the TV thing… like blinking or throat clearing, I don’t think that’s what people complain about. Mostly they brought me in because of the warehouse.\n\nI keep doing these things there, like I freeze with my arms up by the forklift, or I start copying the beeping sounds and the hand signals. And then I move the boxes into these strange little patterns, even when everyone’s yelling at me to stop. It’s like that’s the main thing everybody noticed. The beeping kind of gets stuck in the air there—sorry, I know you asked about home. I just don’t really notice it the same way at home.\n\nDoctor: When you're at home or in other places outside the warehouse, do you ever notice yourself making sudden movements—like blinking, shrugging, or facial twitches—or sudden sounds like grunting or clearing your throat without meaning to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about blinking or grunting, not really… I mean, nobody at home says that’s the problem.\n\nThey brought me in more because at the warehouse I keep doing those things, like I’ll freeze with my arms up near the forklift, or I start copying the beeping and the hand signals. And then with the boxes, I move them into these weird little patterns even when people are yelling at me to stop. It’s mostly that stuff. The warehouse just kind of gets… loud and all lined up wrong, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Even if it doesn’t happen at home, have you ever noticed yourself making sudden movements like blinking, shrugging, or facial twitches, or sudden sounds like grunting or throat clearing in other places outside of work—like in a store, on the street, or somewhere else?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Like, in a store I’m usually just trying to get in and out, and the lights are too much sometimes—well, not the lights, I mean, I just don’t pay attention to whether I’m blinking or shrugging.\n\nNobody’s told me I’m doing that kind of thing, like grunting or clearing my throat. I don’t think so. I mostly only hear about the stuff at the warehouse, so outside of that I don’t really know.\n\nDoctor: When you're about to make those beeping sounds or move your arms like you described, do you ever feel a strong urge or a strange sensation in your body that makes you feel like you have to do it?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s like an urge in my body. It’s more like… at the warehouse it just starts happening. I’ll be by the forklift and my arms sort of go up and I freeze there, or I start copying the beeping sounds and the hand signals, and then the boxes—sometimes I start putting them in these little patterns.\n\nPeople yell at me to stop, but it’s hard to break out of it right then. I’m not thinking, “I need to do this because of a feeling,” exactly. It’s just like the beeping and the movements get caught up in me.\n\nDoctor: When you're not at the warehouse—like when you're at home or somewhere else—do you ever feel a strange feeling in your body before you make a sound or move in a way that you can't control?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I don’t notice that kind of thing when I’m home. If I’m on the couch or making food or whatever, I’m not thinking, “oh, there’s that feeling before I move.” It’s more like I’m just doing normal stuff, and then I get distracted by—like my neighbor’s dog barks at the same time every night, which is annoying, but that’s not the same thing.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I get a strange body feeling before making sounds or moving outside the warehouse. The thing people keep bringing up is at work, like with the forklift. I’ll freeze with my arms up, or I end up copying the beeping and the hand signals, and then I start putting boxes in these little patterns.\n\nIt’s weird because everybody’s yelling and it’s like I can hear them, but I’m also stuck on the beeping or the way the boxes line up. At home I’m mostly just… I don’t know, trying to sit still or eat something. Nobody’s said I’m copying them there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeating words, sounds, or movements that you hear or see other people do in places outside of the warehouse—like at a store, on the street, or when you're at home?\n\nPatient: Outside? I don’t know… I keep thinking about the warehouse because that’s what everyone keeps saying. I’ll be standing there with my arms up, and I know it looks stupid or dangerous, but it’s hard to just stop.\n\nSometimes if someone grabs my shoulder or waves me over, I can move, but other times I kind of keep doing it—like the beeping sound, or the hand signal the guy made. Then I’ll go stack boxes in those little patterns, and they’re all yelling, and I know they don’t want it that way, but I’m already in it. It’s like when you start sorting coins by the year and then you remember the state quarters—sorry, I know that’s not what you asked. But yeah, I freeze and copy stuff, and it’s hard to switch back right away.\n\nDoctor: When you find yourself making the beeping sounds or copying the hand signals, does it feel like something you have to do to relieve a thought or feeling, or does it just happen without a clear reason?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s like… to make a thought go away. It’s not like I’m thinking, “I have to beep or something bad happens.” It more just happens, I guess.\n\nLike at the warehouse, I’ll be by the forklift and I freeze with my arms up, or I start copying the beeping and the hand signals, and then the boxes end up in those little patterns even though everyone’s yelling at me to stop. It’s embarrassing because I can hear them, but I’m already doing it. It’s not really clear to me why.\n\nDoctor: Before you make the beeping sounds or move your arms like that, do you ever feel a warning in your body—like a need to do it, an itch, or something building up that goes away once you do the movement or sound?\n\nPatient: I don’t really notice an itch or anything like that first. It’s more like… I’m just there, and then I’m doing it. Like by the forklift, I’ll freeze with my arms up, or I’m copying the beeping, or doing the hand signals, and people are yelling at me to stop but I’m already sort of caught in it.\n\nAnd then the boxes—sometimes I start putting them in these weird little patterns, not how they’re supposed to go. It’s not like I feel better after, exactly. It just happens, and then it’s hard to stop. The warehouse lights are really bright too, they buzz sometimes like those old school lights, the kind in classrooms—sorry. But yeah, no warning I can point to.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work at the warehouse, do you ever find yourself copying sounds you hear or movements you see other people make, even if you don’t mean to?\n\nPatient: I mean, mostly they brought me in because of the warehouse stuff, I think. Like freezing with my arms up by the forklift, or copying the beeping sounds and the hand signals, and then I’m moving boxes into these little patterns even while people are yelling at me to stop.\n\nOutside of work… I don’t know, I’m trying to think. The forklift beeps are kind of like when a microwave keeps going and nobody opens it, which is annoying—sorry. But the big thing people keep pointing at is what happens at work. That’s where it gets bad and everybody notices.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself making sudden sounds or movements, like blinking, clearing your throat, or shrugging your shoulders, when you're not at work—like when you're at home watching TV or talking with someone?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know about the TV thing… like blinking or throat clearing, I don’t think that’s what people complain about. Mostly they brought me in because of the warehouse.\n\nI keep doing these things there, like I freeze with my arms up by the forklift, or I start copying the beeping sounds and the hand signals. And then I move the boxes into these strange little patterns, even when everyone’s yelling at me to stop. It’s like that’s the main thing everybody noticed. The beeping kind of gets stuck in the air there—sorry, I know you asked about home. I just don’t really notice it the same way at home.\n\nDoctor: When you're at home or in other places outside the warehouse, do you ever notice yourself making sudden movements—like blinking, shrugging, or facial twitches—or sudden sounds like grunting or clearing your throat without meaning to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about blinking or grunting, not really… I mean, nobody at home says that’s the problem.\n\nThey brought me in more because at the warehouse I keep doing those things, like I’ll freeze with my arms up near the forklift, or I start copying the beeping and the hand signals. And then with the boxes, I move them into these weird little patterns even when people are yelling at me to stop. It’s mostly that stuff. The warehouse just kind of gets… loud and all lined up wrong, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Even if it doesn’t happen at home, have you ever noticed yourself making sudden movements like blinking, shrugging, or facial twitches, or sudden sounds like grunting or throat clearing in other places outside of work—like in a store, on the street, or somewhere else?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Like, in a store I’m usually just trying to get in and out, and the lights are too much sometimes—well, not the lights, I mean, I just don’t pay attention to whether I’m blinking or shrugging.\n\nNobody’s told me I’m doing that kind of thing, like grunting or clearing my throat. I don’t think so. I mostly only hear about the stuff at the warehouse, so outside of that I don’t really know.\n\nDoctor: When you're about to make those beeping sounds or move your arms like you described, do you ever feel a strong urge or a strange sensation in your body that makes you feel like you have to do it?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s like an urge in my body. It’s more like… at the warehouse it just starts happening. I’ll be by the forklift and my arms sort of go up and I freeze there, or I start copying the beeping sounds and the hand signals, and then the boxes—sometimes I start putting them in these little patterns.\n\nPeople yell at me to stop, but it’s hard to break out of it right then. I’m not thinking, “I need to do this because of a feeling,” exactly. It’s just like the beeping and the movements get caught up in me.\n\nDoctor: When you're not at the warehouse—like when you're at home or somewhere else—do you ever feel a strange feeling in your body before you make a sound or move in a way that you can't control?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I don’t notice that kind of thing when I’m home. If I’m on the couch or making food or whatever, I’m not thinking, “oh, there’s that feeling before I move.” It’s more like I’m just doing normal stuff, and then I get distracted by—like my neighbor’s dog barks at the same time every night, which is annoying, but that’s not the same thing.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I get a strange body feeling before making sounds or moving outside the warehouse. Or if I do, I haven’t noticed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeating words, sounds, or movements that you hear or see other people do in places outside of the warehouse—like at a store, on the street, or when you're at home?\n\nPatient: I mean… outside the warehouse, I don’t really know. The thing people keep bringing up is at work, like with the forklift. I’ll freeze with my arms up, or I end up copying the beeping and the hand signals, and then I start putting boxes in these little patterns.\n\nIt’s weird because everybody’s yelling and it’s like I can hear them, but I’m also stuck on the beeping or the way the boxes line up. At home I’m mostly just… I don’t know, trying to sit still or eat something. Nobody’s said I’m copying them there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeating words, sounds, or movements that you hear or see other people do in places outside of the warehouse—like at a store, on the street, or when you're at home?\n\nPatient: Outside? Yeah, I mean… like I said, not so much when I’m just walking around or watching people. It’s more at work, at the warehouse. That’s why they brought me here, I guess.

    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF84.3, F98.4, F98.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, thank you for coming in. What brings you here today?\n\nPatient: Uh… I guess they brought me in because of stuff at the warehouse. Like, I’ll be by the forklift and just kind of freeze there with my arms up, and people get mad because it’s not safe, but the beeping gets stuck in my head, you know? Those forklifts beep like the microwave my aunt used to have, except hers had this cracked button and—\n\nAnyway, I guess I also copy the beeping sounds and the hand signals the guys do. And sometimes I move boxes into these little patterns, not how they want them stacked. They yell at me to stop, but I’m already doing it, and then it’s hard to… switch back, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about those times when you freeze by the forklift? Are you aware of what’s happening when it happens, and can you move if someone tries to redirect you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… I’m there, I think. I can hear them yelling sometimes, like “move” or “get back,” but it’s like the forklift beeping gets lined up with my arms, if that makes sense. I’ll be standing there with my arms up, and I know it looks stupid or dangerous, but it’s hard to just stop.\n\nSometimes if someone grabs my shoulder or waves me over, I can move, but other times I kind of keep doing it—like the beeping sound, or the hand signal the guy made. Then I’ll go stack boxes in those little patterns, and they’re all yelling, and I know they don’t want it that way, but I’m already in it. It’s like when you start sorting coins by the year and then you remember the state quarters—sorry, I know that’s not what you asked. But yeah, I freeze and copy stuff, and it’s hard to switch back right away.\n\nDoctor: When you find yourself making the beeping sounds or copying the hand signals, does it feel like something you have to do to relieve a thought or feeling, or does it just happen without a clear reason?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s like… to make a thought go away. It’s not like I’m thinking, “I have to beep or something bad happens.” It more just happens, I guess.\n\nLike at the warehouse, I’ll be by the forklift and I freeze with my arms up, or I start copying the beeping and the hand signals, and then the boxes end up in those little patterns even though everyone’s yelling at me to stop. It’s embarrassing because I can hear them, but I’m already doing it. It’s not really clear to me why.\n\nDoctor: Before you make the beeping sounds or move your arms like that, do you ever feel a warning in your body—like a need to do it, an itch, or something building up that goes away once you do the movement or sound?\n\nPatient: I don’t really notice an itch or anything like that first. It’s more like… I’m just there, and then I’m doing it. Like by the forklift, I’ll freeze with my arms up, or I’m copying the beeping, or doing the hand signals, and people are yelling at me to stop but I’m already sort of caught in it.\n\nAnd then the boxes—sometimes I start putting them in these weird little patterns, not how they’re supposed to go. It’s not like I feel better after, exactly. It just happens, and then it’s hard to stop. The warehouse lights are really bright too, they buzz sometimes like those old school lights, the kind in classrooms—sorry. But yeah, no warning I can point to.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work at the warehouse, do you ever find yourself copying sounds you hear or movements you see other people make, even if you don’t mean to?\n\nPatient: I mean, mostly they brought me in because of the warehouse stuff, I think. Like freezing with my arms up by the forklift, or copying the beeping sounds and the hand signals, and then I’m moving boxes into these little patterns even while people are yelling at me to stop.\n\nOutside of work… I don’t know, I’m trying to think. The forklift beeps are kind of like when a microwave keeps going and nobody opens it, which is annoying—sorry. But the big thing people keep pointing at is what happens at work. That’s where it gets bad and everybody notices.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself making sudden sounds or movements, like blinking, clearing your throat, or shrugging your shoulders, when you're not at work—like when you're at home watching TV or talking with someone?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know about the TV thing… like blinking or throat clearing, I don’t think that’s what people complain about. Mostly they brought me in because of the warehouse.\n\nI keep doing these things there, like I freeze with my arms up by the forklift, or I start copying the beeping sounds and the hand signals. And then I move the boxes into these strange little patterns, even when everyone’s yelling at me to stop. It’s like that’s the main thing everybody noticed. The beeping kind of gets stuck in the air there—sorry, I know you asked about home. I just don’t really notice it the same way at home.\n\nDoctor: When you're at home or in other places outside the warehouse, do you ever notice yourself making sudden movements—like blinking, shrugging, or facial twitches—or sudden sounds like grunting or clearing your throat without meaning to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about blinking or grunting, not really… I mean, nobody at home says that’s the problem.\n\nThey brought me in more because at the warehouse I keep doing those things, like I’ll freeze with my arms up near the forklift, or I start copying the beeping and the hand signals. And then with the boxes, I move them into these weird little patterns even when people are yelling at me to stop. It’s mostly that stuff. The warehouse just kind of gets… loud and all lined up wrong, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Even if it doesn’t happen at home, have you ever noticed yourself making sudden movements like blinking, shrugging, or facial twitches, or sudden sounds like grunting or throat clearing in other places outside of work—like in a store, on the street, or somewhere else?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Like, in a store I’m usually just trying to get in and out, and the lights are too much sometimes—well, not the lights, I mean, I just don’t pay attention to whether I’m blinking or shrugging.\n\nNobody’s told me I’m doing that kind of thing, like grunting or clearing my throat. I don’t think so. I mostly only hear about the stuff at the warehouse, so outside of that I don’t really know.\n\nDoctor: When you're about to make those beeping sounds or move your arms like you described, do you ever feel a strong urge or a strange sensation in your body that makes you feel like you have to do it?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s like an urge in my body. It’s more like… at the warehouse it just starts happening. I’ll be by the forklift and my arms sort of go up and I freeze there, or I start copying the beeping sounds and the hand signals, and then the boxes—sometimes I start putting them in these little patterns.\n\nPeople yell at me to stop, but it’s hard to break out of it right then. I’m not thinking, “I need to do this because of a feeling,” exactly. It’s just like the beeping and the movements get caught up in me.\n\nDoctor: When you're not at the warehouse—like when you're at home or somewhere else—do you ever feel a strange feeling in your body before you make a sound or move in a way that you can't control?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I don’t notice that kind of thing when I’m home. If I’m on the couch or making food or whatever, I’m not thinking, “oh, there’s that feeling before I move.” It’s more like I’m just doing normal stuff, and then I get distracted by—like my neighbor’s dog barks at the same time every night, which is annoying, but that’s not the same thing.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I get a strange body feeling before making sounds or moving outside the warehouse. Or if I do, I haven’t noticed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeating words, sounds, or movements that you hear or see other people do in places outside of the warehouse—like at a store, on the street, or when you're at home?\n\nPatient: I mean… outside the warehouse, I don’t really know. The thing people keep bringing up is at work, like with the forklift. I’ll freeze with my arms up, or I end up copying the beeping and the hand signals, and then I start putting boxes in these little patterns.\n\nIt’s weird because everybody’s yelling and it’s like I can hear them, but I’m also stuck on the beeping or the way the boxes line up. At home I’m mostly just… I don’t know, trying to sit still or eat something. Nobody’s said I’m copying them there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeating words, sounds, or movements that you hear or see other people do in places outside of the warehouse—like at a store, on the street, or when you're at home?\n\nPatient: Outside? I don’t know… I keep thinking about the warehouse because that’s what everyone keeps saying. Like by the forklift I’ll just freeze with my arms up, or I start making the beeping sounds back, doing the hand signals, and then the boxes end up in these little patterns.\n\nIt’s not like I’m trying to make a joke or anything. People yell at me to stop, and I hear them, but I’m kind of stuck on it. At a store or home… I can’t really think of a time like that. It’s mostly the warehouse stuff they brought me in for.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeating words, phrases, or movements that you hear or see other people do — like on TV, in a store, or when you're talking with someone?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess that’s kind of why they brought me in. It’s mostly at the warehouse, not like… I don’t know, TV doesn’t really do that to me, I don’t think. But by the forklift, I’ll freeze with my arms up, or I start copying the beeping sounds, and sometimes the hand signals people are doing.\n\nAnd then the boxes… I start moving them into these little patterns, like they have to line up right, even when everyone’s yelling at me to stop. It’s embarrassing because I hear them, but I’m sort of stuck on the beeping or the way it looks, and then it keeps going.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeating words, phrases, or movements that you hear or see other people do in everyday situations — like when you're talking with someone, walking down the street, or watching people in public?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about like… walking down the street. I don’t really pay attention to people that way, or maybe I do and then I forget. The main thing is still the warehouse.\n\nThey said I keep doing this stuff by the forklift—like I freeze with my arms up, and I copy the beeping sounds, or the hand signals people are making. And then I’ll start moving boxes into these weird little patterns, like they have to sit a certain way, even when everyone’s yelling at me to stop. I hear them, but it’s like I’m already caught in it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeating words, phrases, or movements that you hear or see other people do in everyday situations — like when you're talking with someone, walking down the street, or watching people in public?\n\nPatient: I know you asked like everyday places, but I keep going back to the warehouse because that’s where it happens, or where everybody says it happens. By the forklift I’ll just kind of stop with my arms up, and then the beeping sound gets in my head and I start doing it back.\n\nAnd the hand signals too, like if somebody motions, I copy it, and then I’m moving boxes into these little patterns… little rows or shapes, I don’t know. They’re yelling at me to stop, and I hear it, but I’m already stuck on making it look right. Outside of that, like on the street, I can’t really say.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeating words, phrases, or movements that you hear or see other people do — like when you're talking with someone, walking down the street, or watching people in public?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… like I said, not so much when I’m just walking around or watching people. It’s more at work, at the warehouse. That’s why they brought me here, I guess.\n\nBy the forklift, I’ll just kind of freeze with my arms up, and then the beeping gets stuck and I start making the sound back. Or if someone does those hand signals, I copy them without really meaning to. And then I start moving boxes into these little patterns, like rows or shapes, and people are yelling at me to stop, but I’m already… I don’t know, caught up in it.\n\nIt’s embarrassing. I know you’re asking about regular places too, but that’s the place it keeps happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeating words, phrases, or movements that you hear or see other people do — like when you're talking with someone, walking down the street, or watching people in public?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I already kind of said it, but it’s mostly at the warehouse, not like… I don’t know, people on the sidewalk or whatever. The forklift area, that’s where everyone keeps saying I do it.\n\nI’ll freeze there with my arms up, like I’m stuck, and then the beeping starts and I make the beeping sound back. Or somebody does a hand signal and I copy it, like it just happens. Then I start putting the boxes into these little patterns, rows and shapes and stuff, even when they’re yelling at me to stop. I hear them yelling, but I’m focused on the boxes, like they have to be right.\n\nIt’s weird because I don’t go in thinking, “I’m gonna do that.” It just sort of takes over.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeating words, phrases, or movements that you hear or see other people do — even in small, everyday moments, like when someone says a word and it kind of sticks in your head and you say it back without meaning to?\n\nPatient: Yeah, kind of… I don’t know about just a word in a conversation, like maybe that happens but I don’t notice it as much. The thing people keep talking about is the warehouse.\n\nI guess that’s why they brought me in, because I’ll be by the forklift and just freeze with my arms up, like I’m waiting for something, and then the beeping starts and I make the sound back. And if someone does a hand signal, I’ll copy it before I even think. Then I start moving boxes into these weird little patterns, rows and shapes, even when everyone’s yelling at me to stop.\n\nI know it sounds like I’m not answering it exactly, but that’s where it keeps happening.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Yeah… I guess so, but it’s not like just repeating a word at the grocery store or something. I mean, maybe that happens and I don’t catch it. The main thing is at the warehouse.

Outside the warehouse… I don’t know, maybe, but that’s not really what they keep getting on me about. Like TV, I’ll be watching and then forget what I’m watching because I’m thinking about work, or the boxes, and how they’re supposed to line up, and then my cousin says I’m not listening.

But the copying thing, yeah, it’s mostly there. By the forklift I’ll just kind of stop, arms up, like I’m stuck in that position, and if the beeping starts I’ll make the sound back. Or if one of the guys gives a hand signal, I copy it, like my hands do it before I decide anything. Then I start putting boxes into patterns, rows and shapes, and people are yelling at me to stop but I keep doing it.

So outside of that… I’m not sure. I don’t notice it as much. It’s the warehouse stuff that got bad.
